RULE 2: When mstatus.TVM=1, an attempt to execute HINVAL.GVMA in S-mode or HS-mode raises an illegal-instruction exception.

RULE 3: SFENCE.W.INVAL is unaffected by mstatus.TVM and is always permitted in S-mode and VS-mode.

RULE 4: SFENCE.INVAL.IR is unaffected by mstatus.TVM and is always permitted in S-mode and VS-mode.

RULE 5: SFENCE.W.INVAL does not need to be trapped when mstatus.TVM=1 because it has only ordering effects and no visible side effects.

RULE 6: SFENCE.INVAL.IR does not need to be trapped when mstatus.TVM=1 because it has only ordering effects and no visible side effects.

RULE 7: When mstatus.TVM=1, trapping SINVAL.VMA is sufficient to enable emulation of the intended overall TLB maintenance functionality.

The following description:

- The hstatus register provides facilities analogous to the mstatus register for tracking and controlling the exception behavior of a VS-mode guest.

- The hstatus fields VTSR, VTW, and VTVM are defined analogously to the mstatus fields TSR, TW, and TVM, but affect execution only in VS-mode and cause virtual-instruction exceptions instead of illegal-instruction exceptions.

- When hstatus.VTW=1, and assuming mstatus.TW=0, an attempt in VS-mode to execute WFI raises a virtual-instruction exception if the WFI does not complete within an implementation-specific, bounded time limit.

- An implementation may have WFI always raise a virtual-instruction exception in VS-mode when hstatus.VTW=1 and mstatus.TW=0, even if there are pending globally-disabled interrupts when the instruction is executed.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: The mstatus register provides the model for the analogous hstatus facilities that track and control the exception behavior of a VS-mode guest.

RULE 2: The mstatus.TSR field is analogous to hstatus.VTSR, but hstatus.VTSR affects execution only in VS-mode and causes virtual-instruction exceptions instead of the illegal-instruction exceptions associated with mstatus.TSR.

RULE 3: The mstatus.TW field is analogous to hstatus.VTW, but hstatus.VTW affects execution only in VS-mode and causes virtual-instruction exceptions instead of the illegal-instruction exceptions associated with mstatus.TW.

RULE 4: The mstatus.TVM field is analogous to hstatus.VTVM, but hstatus.VTVM affects execution only in VS-mode and causes virtual-instruction exceptions instead of the illegal-instruction exceptions associated with mstatus.TVM.

RULE 5: When mstatus.TW=0 and hstatus.VTW=1, an attempt in VS-mode to execute WFI raises a virtual-instruction exception if WFI does not complete within an implementation-specific, bounded time limit.

Remember, output must be ASCII-only text.

The following description:

- When the hypervisor extension is implemented and MXLEN=64, the hypervisor extension adds the MPV and GVA fields to the machine-level mstatus CSR.

- When the hypervisor extension is implemented and MXLEN=32, the hypervisor extension adds MPV and GVA to mstatush, not to mstatus. The format of mstatus is unchanged for RV32.

- Whenever a trap is taken into M-mode, the implementation writes the MPV bit. Just as the MPP field is set to the nominal privilege mode at the time of the trap, MPV is set to the value of the virtualization mode V at the time of the trap.

- When an MRET instruction is executed, the virtualization mode V is set to MPV, unless MPP=3, in which case V remains 0.

- Whenever a trap is taken into M-mode, the implementation writes the GVA field.

- For any breakpoint, address-misaligned, access-fault, page-fault, or guest-page-fault trap that writes a guest virtual address to mtval, GVA is set to 1.

- For any other trap into M-mode, GVA is set to 0.

- The TSR and TVM fields of mstatus affect execution only in HS-mode, not in VS-mode.

- The TW field of mstatus affects execution in all modes except M-mode.

- Setting TVM=1 prevents HS-mode from accessing hgatp or executing HFENCE.GVMA or HINVAL.GVMA.

- Setting TVM=1 has no effect on accesses to vsatp or instructions HFENCE.VVMA or HINVAL.VVMA.

- Setting TVM=1 does not cause traps for accesses to vsatp, instructions HFENCE.VVMA or HINVAL.VVMA, or any actions taken in VS-mode.

- The hypervisor extension changes the behavior of the Modify Privilege field, MPRV, of mstatus.

- When MPRV=0, translation and protection behave as normal.

- When MPRV=1, explicit memory accesses are translated and protected, and endianness is applied, as though the current virtualization mode were set to MPV and the current nominal privilege mode were set to MPP.

Effect of MPRV on the translation and protection of explicit memory accesses:

| MPRV | MPV | MPP | Effect |
|------|-----|-----|--------|
| 0 | - | - | Normal access; current privilege mode applies. |
| 1 | 0 | 0 | U-level access with HS-level translation and protection only. |
| 1 | 0 | 1 | HS-level access with HS-level translation and protection only. |
| 1 | - | 3 | M-level access with no translation. |
| 1 | 1 | 0 | VU-level access with two-stage translation and protection. The HS-level MXR bit makes any executable page readable. vsstatus.MXR makes readable those pages marked executable at the VS translation stage, but only if readable at the guest-physical translation stage. |
| 1 | 1 | 1 | VS-level access with two-stage translation and protection. The HS-level MXR bit makes any executable page readable. vsstatus.MXR makes readable those pages marked executable at the VS translation stage, but only if readable at the guest-physical translation stage. vsstatus.SUM applies instead of the HS-level SUM bit. |

- MPRV does not affect the virtual-machine load/store instructions HLV, HLVX, and HSV.

- The explicit loads and stores of HLV, HLVX, and HSV always act as though V=1 and the nominal privilege mode were hstatus.SPVP, overriding MPRV.

- The mstatus register is a superset of the HS-level sstatus register but is not a superset of vsstatus.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: When the hypervisor extension is implemented and MXLEN=64, the hypervisor extension adds the MPV field to the machine-level mstatus CSR.
RULE 2: When the hypervisor extension is implemented and MXLEN=64, the hypervisor extension adds the GVA field to the machine-level mstatus CSR.
RULE 3: When the hypervisor extension is implemented and MXLEN=32, MPV is added to mstatush rather than mstatus.
RULE 4: When the hypervisor extension is implemented and MXLEN=32, GVA is added to mstatush rather than mstatus.
RULE 5: When the hypervisor extension is implemented and MXLEN=32, the RV32 format of mstatus is unchanged.
RULE 6: When the hypervisor extension is implemented and MXLEN=64, the implementation writes mstatus.MPV whenever a trap is taken into M-mode.
RULE 7: Whenever a trap is taken into M-mode, mstatus.MPP is set to the nominal privilege mode at the time of the trap.
RULE 8: When the hypervisor extension is implemented and MXLEN=64, mstatus.MPV is set to the value of virtualization mode V at the time a trap is taken into M-mode.
RULE 9: When MRET is executed and mstatus.MPP is not 3, virtualization mode V is set to MPV.
RULE 10: When MRET is executed and mstatus.MPP is 3, virtualization mode V remains 0.
RULE 11: When the hypervisor extension is implemented and MXLEN=64, the implementation writes mstatus.GVA whenever a trap is taken into M-mode.
RULE 12: When the hypervisor extension is implemented and MXLEN=64, mstatus.GVA is set to 1 for a breakpoint trap into M-mode that writes a guest virtual address to mtval.
RULE 13: When the hypervisor extension is implemented and MXLEN=64, mstatus.GVA is set to 1 for an address-misaligned trap into M-mode that writes a guest virtual address to mtval.
RULE 14: When the hypervisor extension is implemented and MXLEN=64, mstatus.GVA is set to 1 for an access-fault trap into M-mode that writes a guest virtual address to mtval.
RULE 15: When the hypervisor extension is implemented and MXLEN=64, mstatus.GVA is set to 1 for a page-fault trap into M-mode that writes a guest virtual address to mtval.
RULE 16: When the hypervisor extension is implemented and MXLEN=64, mstatus.GVA is set to 1 for a guest-page-fault trap into M-mode that writes a guest virtual address to mtval.
RULE 17: When the hypervisor extension is implemented and MXLEN=64, mstatus.GVA is set to 0 for any trap into M-mode that does not both belong to the breakpoint, address-misaligned, access-fault, page-fault, or guest-page-fault classes and write a guest virtual address to mtval.
RULE 18: The mstatus.TSR field affects execution only in HS-mode and not in VS-mode.
RULE 19: The mstatus.TVM field affects execution only in HS-mode and not in VS-mode.
RULE 20: The mstatus.TW field affects execution in every mode except M-mode.
RULE 21: Setting mstatus.TVM to 1 prevents HS-mode from accessing hgatp.
RULE 22: Setting mstatus.TVM to 1 prevents HS-mode from executing HFENCE.GVMA.
RULE 23: Setting mstatus.TVM to 1 prevents HS-mode from executing HINVAL.GVMA.
RULE 24: Setting mstatus.TVM to 1 has no effect on accesses to vsatp.
RULE 25: Setting mstatus.TVM to 1 has no effect on the HFENCE.VVMA instruction.
RULE 26: Setting mstatus.TVM to 1 has no effect on the HINVAL.VVMA instruction.
RULE 27: Setting mstatus.TVM to 1 does not cause traps for accesses to vsatp.
RULE 28: Setting mstatus.TVM to 1 does not cause traps for the HFENCE.VVMA instruction.
RULE 29: Setting mstatus.TVM to 1 does not cause traps for the HINVAL.VVMA instruction.
RULE 30: Setting mstatus.TVM to 1 does not cause traps for any actions taken in VS-mode.
RULE 31: When the hypervisor extension is implemented, it changes the behavior of the Modify Privilege field mstatus.MPRV.
RULE 32: When mstatus.MPRV is 0, translation and protection for explicit memory accesses behave normally regardless of MPV and mstatus.MPP.
RULE 33: When mstatus.MPRV is 0, the current privilege mode applies to explicit memory accesses regardless of MPV and mstatus.MPP.
RULE 34: When mstatus.MPRV is 1, explicit memory accesses are translated and protected as though the current virtualization mode were MPV and the current nominal privilege mode were mstatus.MPP.
RULE 35: When mstatus.MPRV is 1, endianness is applied to explicit memory accesses as though the current virtualization mode were MPV and the current nominal privilege mode were mstatus.MPP.
RULE 36: When mstatus.MPRV is 1, MPV is 0, and mstatus.MPP is 0, explicit memory accesses act as U-level accesses.
RULE 37: When mstatus.MPRV is 1, MPV is 0, and mstatus.MPP is 0, explicit memory accesses use only HS-level translation and protection.
RULE 38: When mstatus.MPRV is 1, MPV is 0, and mstatus.MPP is 1, explicit memory accesses act as HS-level accesses.
RULE 39: When mstatus.MPRV is 1, MPV is 0, and mstatus.MPP is 1, explicit memory accesses use only HS-level translation and protection.
RULE 40: When mstatus.MPRV is 1 and mstatus.MPP is 3, explicit memory accesses act as M-level accesses regardless of MPV.
RULE 41: When mstatus.MPRV is 1 and mstatus.MPP is 3, explicit memory accesses use no address translation regardless of MPV.
RULE 42: When mstatus.MPRV is 1, MPV is 1, and mstatus.MPP is 0, explicit memory accesses act as VU-level accesses.
RULE 43: When mstatus.MPRV is 1, MPV is 1, and mstatus.MPP is 0, explicit memory accesses use two-stage translation and protection.
RULE 44: When mstatus.MPRV is 1, MPV is 1, and mstatus.MPP is 0, the HS-level MXR bit makes any executable page readable for explicit memory accesses.
RULE 45: When mstatus.MPRV is 1, MPV is 1, and mstatus.MPP is 0, vsstatus.MXR makes pages marked executable at the VS translation stage readable for explicit memory accesses only if those pages are readable at the guest-physical translation stage.
RULE 46: When mstatus.MPRV is 1, MPV is 1, and mstatus.MPP is 1, explicit memory accesses act as VS-level accesses.
RULE 47: When mstatus.MPRV is 1, MPV is 1, and mstatus.MPP is 1, explicit memory accesses use two-stage translation and protection.
RULE 48: When mstatus.MPRV is 1, MPV is 1, and mstatus.MPP is 1, the HS-level MXR bit makes any executable page readable for explicit memory accesses.
RULE 49: When mstatus.MPRV is 1, MPV is 1, and mstatus.MPP is 1, vsstatus.MXR makes pages marked executable at the VS translation stage readable for explicit memory accesses only if those pages are readable at the guest-physical translation stage.
RULE 50: When mstatus.MPRV is 1, MPV is 1, and mstatus.MPP is 1, vsstatus.SUM applies to explicit memory accesses instead of the HS-level SUM bit.
RULE 51: The mstatus.MPRV field does not affect the HLV instruction.
RULE 52: The mstatus.MPRV field does not affect the HLVX instruction.
RULE 53: The mstatus.MPRV field does not affect the HSV instruction.
RULE 54: The explicit load performed by HLV always acts as though virtualization mode V were 1, overriding mstatus.MPRV.
RULE 55: The explicit load performed by HLV always acts as though the nominal privilege mode were hstatus.SPVP, overriding mstatus.MPRV.
RULE 56: The explicit load performed by HLVX always acts as though virtualization mode V were 1, overriding mstatus.MPRV.

The following description:

- When V=1, a virtual-instruction exception (code 22) is normally raised instead of an illegal-instruction exception if the attempted instruction is HS-qualified but is prevented from executing when V=1 either due to insufficient privilege or because the instruction is expressly disabled by a supervisor or hypervisor CSR such as scounteren or hcounteren. An instruction is HS-qualified if it would be valid to execute in HS-mode, for some values of the instruction's register operands, assuming fields TSR and TVM of CSR mstatus are both zero.

- In VS-mode or VU-mode, a virtual-instruction exception is raised for attempts to access an implemented non-high-half hypervisor CSR or VS CSR when the same access (read/write) would be allowed in HS-mode, assuming mstatus.TVM=0.

- In VS-mode or VU-mode, if XLEN=32, a virtual-instruction exception is raised for attempts to access an implemented high-half hypervisor CSR or high-half VS CSR when the same access (read/write) to the CSR's low-half partner would be allowed in HS-mode, assuming mstatus.TVM=0.

- In VU-mode, a virtual-instruction exception is raised for attempts to execute WFI when mstatus.TW=0.

- In VU-mode, a virtual-instruction exception is raised for attempts to access an implemented non-high-half supervisor CSR when the same access (read/write) would be allowed in HS-mode, assuming mstatus.TVM=0.

- In VU-mode, if XLEN=32, a virtual-instruction exception is raised for attempts to access an implemented high-half supervisor CSR when the same access to the CSR's low-half partner would be allowed in HS-mode, assuming mstatus.TVM=0.

- In VS-mode, a virtual-instruction exception is raised for attempts to execute WFI when hstatus.VTW=1 and mstatus.TW=0, unless the instruction completes within an implementation-specific, bounded time.

- Because TSR and TVM in mstatus are intended to impact only S-mode (HS-mode), they are ignored for determining exceptions in VS-mode.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: When V=1, a virtual-instruction exception with code 22 is normally raised instead of an illegal-instruction exception if an attempted instruction would be valid to execute in HS-mode for some values of its register operands assuming mstatus.TSR=0 and mstatus.TVM=0, but execution when V=1 is prevented either by insufficient privilege or because a supervisor or hypervisor CSR such as scounteren or hcounteren expressly disables the instruction.

RULE 2: In VS-mode or VU-mode, an attempt to access an implemented non-high-half hypervisor CSR or VS CSR raises a virtual-instruction exception when the same read or write access would be allowed in HS-mode assuming mstatus.TVM=0.

RULE 3: In VS-mode or VU-mode when XLEN=32, an attempt to access an implemented high-half hypervisor CSR or high-half VS CSR raises a virtual-instruction exception when the same read or write access to the CSR's low-half partner would be allowed in HS-mode assuming mstatus.TVM=0.

RULE 4: In VU-mode, an attempt to execute WFI raises a virtual-instruction exception when mstatus.TW=0.

RULE 5: In VU-mode, an attempt to access an implemented non-high-half supervisor CSR raises a virtual-instruction exception when the same read or write access would be allowed in HS-mode assuming mstatus.TVM=0.

RULE 6: In VU-mode when XLEN=32, an attempt to access an implemented high-half supervisor CSR raises a virtual-instruction exception when the same access to the CSR's low-half partner would be allowed in HS-mode assuming mstatus.TVM=0.

RULE 7: In VS-mode, an attempt to execute WFI raises a virtual-instruction exception when hstatus.VTW=1 and mstatus.TW=0 unless the instruction completes within an implementation-specific, bounded time.

RULE 8: The mstatus.TSR and mstatus.TVM fields are intended to impact only S-mode, which is HS-mode when the hypervisor extension is active.

RULE 9: The mstatus.TSR and mstatus.TVM fields are ignored when determining exceptions in VS-mode.

The following description:

- When a trap occurs in HS-mode or U-mode, it goes to M-mode, unless delegated by medeleg or mideleg, in which case it goes to HS-mode.

- When a trap occurs in VS-mode or VU-mode, it goes to M-mode, unless delegated by medeleg or mideleg, in which case it goes to HS-mode, unless further delegated by hedeleg or hideleg, in which case it goes to VS-mode.

- When a trap is taken into M-mode:
  - Virtualization mode V gets set to 0.
  - Fields MPV and MPP in mstatus (or mstatush) are set according to Table 52.
  - Fields GVA, MPIE, and MIE in mstatus/mstatush are written.
  - CSRs mepc, mcause, mtval, mtval2, and mtinst are written.

Table 52. Value of mstatus/mstatush fields MPV and MPP after a trap into M-mode.

| Previous Mode | MPV | MPP |
|---------------|-----|-----|
| U-mode        | 0   | 0   |
| HS-mode       | 0   | 1   |
| M-mode        | 0   | 3   |
| VU-mode       | 1   | 0   |
| VS-mode       | 1   | 1   |

- Upon trap return, MPV is ignored when MPP=3.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: When a trap is taken into M-mode and the previous mode was U-mode, the MPV field of mstatus or mstatush is set to 0.

RULE 2: When a trap is taken into M-mode and the previous mode was U-mode, the MPP field of mstatus is set to 0.

RULE 3: When a trap is taken into M-mode and the previous mode was HS-mode, the MPV field of mstatus or mstatush is set to 0.

RULE 4: When a trap is taken into M-mode and the previous mode was HS-mode, the MPP field of mstatus is set to 1.

RULE 5: When a trap is taken into M-mode and the previous mode was M-mode, the MPV field of mstatus or mstatush is set to 0.

RULE 6: When a trap is taken into M-mode and the previous mode was M-mode, the MPP field of mstatus is set to 3.

RULE 7: When a trap is taken into M-mode and the previous mode was VU-mode, the MPV field of mstatus or mstatush is set to 1.

RULE 8: When a trap is taken into M-mode and the previous mode was VU-mode, the MPP field of mstatus is set to 0.

RULE 9: When a trap is taken into M-mode and the previous mode was VS-mode, the MPV field of mstatus or mstatush is set to 1.

RULE 10: When a trap is taken into M-mode and the previous mode was VS-mode, the MPP field of mstatus is set to 1.

RULE 11: When a trap is taken into M-mode, the GVA field of mstatus or mstatush is written.

RULE 12: When a trap is taken into M-mode, the MPIE field of mstatus is written.

RULE 13: When a trap is taken into M-mode, the MIE field of mstatus is written.

RULE 14: Upon trap return, the MPV field of mstatus or mstatush is ignored when the MPP field of mstatus equals 3.

The following description:

1. The MRET instruction is used to return from a trap taken into M-mode.

2. MRET first determines what the new privilege mode will be according to the values of MPP and MPV in mstatus or mstatush, as encoded in Table 52.

3. MRET then, in mstatus/mstatush, sets:
   - MPV=0
   - MPP=0
   - MIE=MPIE
   - MPIE=1

4. Lastly, MRET sets the privilege mode as previously determined.

Table 52 is referenced by the provided text but is not included in it.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: When MRET returns from a trap taken into M-mode, MRET first determines the new privilege mode according to the encoded values of the MPP and MPV fields in mstatus or mstatush.

RULE 2: After determining the new privilege mode, MRET sets the MPV field in mstatus or mstatush to 0.

RULE 3: After determining the new privilege mode, MRET sets the MPP field in mstatus or mstatush to 0.

RULE 4: After determining the new privilege mode, MRET sets the MIE field in mstatus or mstatush equal to the MPIE field.

RULE 5: After determining the new privilege mode, MRET sets the MPIE field in mstatus or mstatush to 1.

RULE 6: After setting MPV to 0, MPP to 0, MIE equal to MPIE, and MPIE to 1 in mstatus or mstatush, MRET sets the privilege mode to the mode previously determined from the encoded MPP and MPV values in mstatus or mstatush.

Remember, output must be ASCII-only text.

The following description:

1. A trap may need to be delivered to the same or to a higher privilege mode upon completion of JALR, C.JALR, or C.JR, but before the instruction at the target of the indirect call/jump was decoded, due to:
   - Asynchronous interrupts.
   - Synchronous exceptions with priority higher than that of a software-check exception with xtval set to "landing pad fault (code=2)".

2. The software-check exception caused by Zicfilp has higher priority than an illegal-instruction exception but lower priority than instruction access-fault.

3. The software-check exception due to the instruction not being an LPAD instruction when ELP is LP_EXPECTED, or a software-check exception caused by the LPAD instruction itself, leads to a trap being delivered to the same or to a higher privilege mode.

4. In such cases, the ELP prior to the trap, the previous ELP, must be preserved by the trap delivery such that it can be restored on a return from the trap.

5. To store the previous ELP state on trap delivery to M-mode, an MPELP bit is provided in the mstatus CSR.

6. To store the previous ELP state on trap delivery to S/HS-mode, an SPELP bit is provided in the mstatus CSR.

7. The SPELP bit in mstatus can be accessed through the sstatus CSR.

8. When a trap is taken into privilege mode x, xPELP is set to ELP and ELP is set to NO_LP_EXPECTED. For trap delivery to M-mode, xPELP is the MPELP bit in mstatus. For trap delivery to S/HS-mode, xPELP is the SPELP bit in mstatus.

9. An MRET or SRET instruction is used to return from a trap in M-mode or S-mode, respectively.

10. When executing an xRET instruction, if the new privilege mode is y, then ELP is set to the value of xPELP if yLPE is 1.

11. When executing an xRET instruction, if the new privilege mode is y and yLPE is not 1, ELP is set to NO_LP_EXPECTED.

12. When executing an xRET instruction, xPELP is set to NO_LP_EXPECTED.

13. The trap handler in privilege mode x must save the xPELP bit before performing an indirect call/jump if xLPE=1.

14. If privilege mode x can respond to interrupts and xLPE=1, then the trap handler should also save the xPELP bit before enabling interrupts.

15. The trap handler in privilege mode x must restore the saved xPELP bit before executing the xRET instruction to return from a trap.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: The mstatus CSR provides the MPELP bit to store the previous ELP state when a trap is delivered to M-mode.

RULE 2: The mstatus CSR provides the SPELP bit to store the previous ELP state when a trap is delivered to S/HS-mode.

RULE 3: The SPELP bit in mstatus is accessible through the sstatus CSR.

RULE 4: If an asynchronous interrupt delivers a trap to M-mode after completion of JALR, C.JALR, or C.JR but before the target instruction is decoded, mstatus.MPELP MUST preserve the ELP value from before the trap so that the value can be restored on return.

RULE 5: If an asynchronous interrupt delivers a trap to S/HS-mode after completion of JALR, C.JALR, or C.JR but before the target instruction is decoded, mstatus.SPELP MUST preserve the ELP value from before the trap so that the value can be restored on return.

RULE 6: If a synchronous exception with priority higher than a software-check exception having xtval set to landing pad fault with code 2 delivers a trap to M-mode after completion of JALR, C.JALR, or C.JR but before the target instruction is decoded, mstatus.MPELP MUST preserve the ELP value from before the trap so that the value can be restored on return.

RULE 7: If a synchronous exception with priority higher than a software-check exception having xtval set to landing pad fault with code 2 delivers a trap to S/HS-mode after completion of JALR, C.JALR, or C.JR but before the target instruction is decoded, mstatus.SPELP MUST preserve the ELP value from before the trap so that the value can be restored on return.

RULE 8: If the instruction is not an LPAD instruction when ELP is LP_EXPECTED and the resulting software-check exception delivers a trap to M-mode, mstatus.MPELP MUST preserve the ELP value from before the trap so that the value can be restored on return.

RULE 9: If the instruction is not an LPAD instruction when ELP is LP_EXPECTED and the resulting software-check exception delivers a trap to S/HS-mode, mstatus.SPELP MUST preserve the ELP value from before the trap so that the value can be restored on return.

RULE 10: If an LPAD instruction causes a software-check exception that delivers a trap to M-mode, mstatus.MPELP MUST preserve the ELP value from before the trap so that the value can be restored on return.

RULE 11: If an LPAD instruction causes a software-check exception that delivers a trap to S/HS-mode, mstatus.SPELP MUST preserve the ELP value from before the trap so that the value can be restored on return.

RULE 12: For trap delivery to M-mode, xPELP is the MPELP bit in mstatus.

RULE 13: For trap delivery to S/HS-mode, xPELP is the SPELP bit in mstatus.

RULE 14: When a trap is taken into M-mode, mstatus.MPELP is set to the value of ELP.

RULE 15: When a trap is taken into M-mode, the trap-delivery operation associated with mstatus.MPELP sets ELP to NO_LP_EXPECTED.

RULE 16: When a trap is taken into S/HS-mode, mstatus.SPELP is set to the value of ELP.

RULE 17: When a trap is taken into S/HS-mode, the trap-delivery operation associated with mstatus.SPELP sets ELP to NO_LP_EXPECTED.

RULE 18: When MRET executes and the new privilege mode is y, ELP is set to the value of mstatus.MPELP if yLPE is 1.

RULE 19: When SRET executes and the new privilege mode is y, ELP is set to the value of mstatus.SPELP if yLPE is 1.

RULE 20: When MRET executes and the new privilege mode is y, ELP is set to NO_LP_EXPECTED instead of the value of mstatus.MPELP if yLPE is not 1.

RULE 21: When SRET executes and the new privilege mode is y, ELP is set to NO_LP_EXPECTED instead of the value of mstatus.SPELP if yLPE is not 1.

RULE 22: When MRET executes, mstatus.MPELP is set to NO_LP_EXPECTED.

RULE 23: When SRET executes, mstatus.SPELP is set to NO_LP_EXPECTED.

RULE 24: An M-mode trap handler MUST save mstatus.MPELP before performing an indirect call or jump if xLPE is 1 for M-mode.

RULE 25: An S/HS-mode trap handler MUST save mstatus.SPELP before performing an indirect call or jump if xLPE is 1 for S/HS-mode.

RULE 26: An M-mode trap handler that can respond to interrupts SHOULD save mstatus.MPELP before enabling interrupts if xLPE is 1 for M-mode.

RULE 27: An S/HS-mode trap handler that can respond to interrupts SHOULD save mstatus.SPELP before enabling interrupts if xLPE is 1 for S/HS-mode.

RULE 28: An M-mode trap handler MUST restore the saved mstatus.MPELP value before executing MRET to return from the trap.

RULE 29: An S/HS-mode trap handler MUST restore the saved mstatus.SPELP value before executing SRET to return from the trap.
======================================================= END - 48 =======================================================

The following description:

- The TVM (Trap Virtual Memory) bit is a WARL field that supports intercepting supervisor virtual-memory management operations.

- When TVM=1, attempts to read or write the satp CSR while executing in S-mode will raise an illegal-instruction exception.

- When TVM=0, attempts to read or write the satp CSR are permitted in S-mode.

- When S-mode is not supported, TVM is read-only 0.

- Trapping satp accesses provides the hooks necessary to lazily populate shadow page tables.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: The TVM bit that controls interception of S-mode satp accesses is a WARL field.

RULE 2: The TVM bit supports intercepting S-mode satp accesses as supervisor virtual-memory management operations.

RULE 3: When TVM=1, an attempt to read the satp CSR while executing in S-mode will raise an illegal-instruction exception.

RULE 4: When TVM=1, an attempt to write the satp CSR while executing in S-mode will raise an illegal-instruction exception.

RULE 5: When TVM=0, an attempt to read the satp CSR is permitted in S-mode.

RULE 6: When TVM=0, an attempt to write the satp CSR is permitted in S-mode.

RULE 7: When S-mode is not supported, the TVM bit that controls S-mode satp access is read-only 0.

RULE 8: Trapping satp accesses provides the hooks necessary to lazily populate shadow page tables.

The following description:

- The satp CSR is an SXLEN-bit read/write register that controls supervisor-mode address translation and protection.

- satp holds:
  - The physical page number (PPN) of the root page table, i.e., its supervisor physical address divided by 4 KiB.
  - An address space identifier (ASID), which facilitates address-translation fences on a per-addressspace basis.
  - The MODE field, which selects the current address-translation scheme.

- When SXLEN=32, satp has the following format:

| Bits  | Field | Type | Width |
|-------|-------|------|-------|
| 31    | MODE  | WARL | 1     |
| 30:22 | ASID  | WARL | 9     |
| 21:0  | PPN   | WARL | 22    |

- When SXLEN=64, for MODE values Bare, Sv39, Sv48, and Sv57, satp has the following format:

| Bits  | Field | Type | Width |
|-------|-------|------|-------|
| 63:60 | MODE  | WARL | 4     |
| 59:44 | ASID  | WARL | 16    |
| 43:0  | PPN   | WARL | 44    |

- For RV32, storing a PPN in satp, rather than a physical address, supports a physical address space larger than 4 GiB.

- The satp.PPN field might not be capable of holding all physical page numbers.

- Some platform standards might place constraints on the values satp.PPN may assume, e.g., by requiring that all physical page numbers corresponding to main memory be representable.

- The ASID and the page table base address are stored in the same CSR to allow the pair to be changed atomically on a context switch. Swapping them non-atomically could pollute the old virtual address space with new translations, or vice versa.

- When MODE=Bare, supervisor virtual addresses are equal to supervisor physical addresses, and there is no additional memory protection beyond the physical memory protection scheme described in Section 3.7.

- To select MODE=Bare, software must write zero to the remaining fields of satp:
  - Bits 30:0 when SXLEN=32.
  - Bits 59:0 when SXLEN=64.

- Attempting to select MODE=Bare with a nonzero pattern in the remaining fields has:
  - An UNSPECIFIED effect on the value that the remaining fields assume.
  - An UNSPECIFIED effect on address translation and protection behavior.

- When SXLEN=32, the satp encodings corresponding to MODE=Bare and ASID[8:7]=3 are designated for custom use.

- When SXLEN=32, the satp encodings corresponding to MODE=Bare and ASID[8:7]!=3 are reserved for future standard use.

- When SXLEN=64, all satp encodings corresponding to MODE=Bare are reserved for future standard use.

- When SXLEN=32, the only valid setting for MODE other than Bare is Sv32, a paged virtual-memory scheme described in Section 12.3.

- When SXLEN=64, the defined paged virtual-memory schemes are Sv39, Sv48, and Sv57, described in Section 12.4, Section 12.5, and Section 12.6, respectively.

- When SXLEN=64, Sv64 will be defined in a later version of the specification.

- When SXLEN=64, the remaining MODE settings are reserved for future use and may define different interpretations of the other fields in satp.

- Implementations are not required to support all MODE settings.

- If satp is written with an unsupported MODE, the entire write has no effect; no fields in satp are modified.

- The encodings of the satp MODE field are:

| SXLEN | Value | Name | Description |
|-------|-------|------|-------------|
| 32    | 0     | Bare | No translation or protection. |
| 32    | 1     | Sv32 | Page-based 32-bit virtual addressing (see Section 12.3). |
| 64    | 0     | Bare | No translation or protection. |
| 64    | 1-7   | -    | Reserved for standard use. |
| 64    | 8     | Sv39 | Page-based 39-bit virtual addressing (see Section 12.4). |
| 64    | 9     | Sv48 | Page-based 48-bit virtual addressing (see Section 12.5). |
| 64    | 10    | Sv57 | Page-based 57-bit virtual addressing (see Section 12.6). |
| 64    | 11    | Sv64 | Reserved for page-based 64-bit virtual addressing. |
| 64    | 12-13 | -    | Reserved for standard use. |
| 64    | 14-15 | -    | Designated for custom use. |

- The number of ASID bits is UNSPECIFIED and may be zero.

- The number of implemented ASID bits, termed ASIDLEN, may be determined by writing one to every bit position in the ASID field, then reading back the value in satp to see which bit positions in the ASID field hold a one.

- The least-significant bits of ASID are implemented first: if ASIDLEN > 0, ASID[ASIDLEN-1:0] is writable.

- The maximal value of ASIDLEN, termed ASIDMAX, is:
  - 9 for Sv32.
  - 16 for Sv39, Sv48, and Sv57.

- The satp CSR is considered active when the effective privilege mode is S-mode or U-mode.

- Executions of the address-translation algorithm may only begin using a given value of satp when satp is active.

- Translations that began while satp was active are not required to complete or terminate when satp is no longer active, unless an SFENCE.VMA instruction matching the address and ASID is executed.

- The SFENCE.VMA instruction must be used to ensure that updates to the address-translation data structures are observed by subsequent implicit reads to those structures by a hart.

- Writing satp does not imply any ordering constraints between page-table updates and subsequent address translations.

- Writing satp does not imply any invalidation of address-translation caches.

- If the new address space's page tables have been modified, or if an ASID is reused, it may be necessary to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction after, or in some cases before, writing satp.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: The satp CSR is an SXLEN-bit read/write register.

RULE 2: The satp CSR controls supervisor-mode address translation and protection.

RULE 3: The satp.PPN field holds the physical page number of the root page table, which is the root page table's supervisor physical address divided by 4 KiB.

RULE 4: The satp.ASID field holds an address-space identifier that facilitates address-translation fences on a per-address-space basis.

RULE 5: The satp.MODE field selects the current address-translation scheme.

RULE 6: When SXLEN=32, satp.MODE is a 1-bit WARL field located at bit 31.

RULE 7: When SXLEN=32, satp.ASID is a 9-bit WARL field located at bits 30:22.

RULE 8: When SXLEN=32, satp.PPN is a 22-bit WARL field located at bits 21:0.

RULE 9: When SXLEN=64 and satp.MODE is Bare, Sv39, Sv48, or Sv57, satp.MODE is a 4-bit WARL field located at bits 63:60.

RULE 10: When SXLEN=64 and satp.MODE is Bare, Sv39, Sv48, or Sv57, satp.ASID is a 16-bit WARL field located at bits 59:44.

RULE 11: When SXLEN=64 and satp.MODE is Bare, Sv39, Sv48, or Sv57, satp.PPN is a 44-bit WARL field located at bits 43:0.

RULE 12: On RV32, storing a physical page number rather than a physical address in satp.PPN supports a physical address space larger than 4 GiB.

RULE 13: The satp.PPN field might not be capable of holding every physical page number.

RULE 14: Platform standards might constrain the values that satp.PPN may assume, such as by requiring satp.PPN to represent every physical page number corresponding to main memory.

RULE 15: satp stores the ASID and page-table base address in the same CSR so that the pair can be changed atomically during a context switch.

RULE 16: Changing the ASID and page-table base address associated with satp non-atomically could pollute the old virtual address space with new translations or pollute the new virtual address space with old translations.

RULE 17: When satp.MODE=Bare, supervisor virtual addresses are equal to supervisor physical addresses.

RULE 18: When satp.MODE=Bare, satp provides no additional memory protection beyond the physical memory protection scheme.

RULE 19: When SXLEN=32, software must write zero to satp bits 30:0 to select satp.MODE=Bare.

RULE 20: When SXLEN=64, software must write zero to satp bits 59:0 to select satp.MODE=Bare.

RULE 21: Attempting to select satp.MODE=Bare with a nonzero pattern in the remaining satp fields has an UNSPECIFIED effect on the values assumed by those remaining fields.

RULE 22: Attempting to select satp.MODE=Bare with a nonzero pattern in the remaining satp fields has an UNSPECIFIED effect on address-translation and protection behavior.

RULE 23: When SXLEN=32, satp encodings with MODE=Bare and ASID[8:7]=3 are designated for custom use.

RULE 24: When SXLEN=32, satp encodings with MODE=Bare and ASID[8:7]!=3 are reserved for future standard use.

RULE 25: When SXLEN=64, all satp encodings corresponding to MODE=Bare are reserved for future standard use.

RULE 26: When SXLEN=32, Sv32 is the only valid satp.MODE setting other than Bare.

RULE 27: When SXLEN=64, satp defines Sv39, Sv48, and Sv57 as paged virtual-memory schemes.

RULE 28: When SXLEN=64, the satp Sv64 address-translation scheme will be defined in a later version of the specification.

RULE 29: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE values reserved for future use may define different interpretations of the other satp fields.

RULE 30: A satp implementation is not required to support every satp.MODE setting.

RULE 31: If satp is written with an unsupported MODE value, the entire write has no effect and no satp field is modified.

RULE 32: When SXLEN=32, satp.MODE value 0 encodes Bare, which provides no address translation or protection.

RULE 33: When SXLEN=32, satp.MODE value 1 encodes Sv32, which provides page-based 32-bit virtual addressing.

RULE 34: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE value 0 encodes Bare, which provides no address translation or protection.

RULE 35: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE values 1 through 7 are reserved for standard use.

RULE 36: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE value 8 encodes Sv39, which provides page-based 39-bit virtual addressing.

RULE 37: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE value 9 encodes Sv48, which provides page-based 48-bit virtual addressing.

RULE 38: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE value 10 encodes Sv57, which provides page-based 57-bit virtual addressing.

RULE 39: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE value 11 is named Sv64 and is reserved for page-based 64-bit virtual addressing.

RULE 40: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE values 12 through 13 are reserved for standard use.

RULE 41: When SXLEN=64, satp.MODE values 14 through 15 are designated for custom use.

RULE 42: The number of ASID bits implemented by satp is UNSPECIFIED.

RULE 43: The number of ASID bits implemented by satp may be zero.

RULE 44: satp.ASIDLEN is the number of implemented bits in the satp.ASID field.

RULE 45: satp.ASIDLEN may be determined by writing one to every bit position in satp.ASID and then reading satp to identify which satp.ASID bit positions retain a one.

RULE 46: satp implements the least-significant bits of the ASID field first.

RULE 47: If satp.ASIDLEN>0, satp.ASID[ASIDLEN-1:0] is writable.

RULE 48: For satp.MODE=Sv32, ASIDMAX, which is the maximum value of satp.ASIDLEN, is 9.

RULE 49: For satp.MODE=Sv39, Sv48, or Sv57, ASIDMAX, which is the maximum value of satp.ASIDLEN, is 16.

RULE 50: The satp CSR is active when the effective privilege mode is S-mode or U-mode.

RULE 51: An execution of the address-translation algorithm may begin using a given satp value only while satp is active.

RULE 52: A translation that began while satp was active is not required to complete or terminate after satp becomes inactive unless an SFENCE.VMA instruction matching the translation's address and ASID is executed.

RULE 53: For address translation controlled by satp, an SFENCE.VMA instruction must be used to ensure that updates to address-translation data structures are observed by subsequent implicit reads of those structures by a hart.

RULE 54: Writing satp does not imply any ordering constraints between page-table updates and subsequent address translations.

RULE 55: Writing satp does not imply any invalidation of address-translation caches.

RULE 56: If the page tables for a new address space selected through satp have been modified, it may be necessary to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction after writing satp or, in some cases, before writing satp.

RULE 57: If an ASID written to satp is reused, it may be necessary to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction after writing satp or, in some cases, before writing satp.

The following description:

- If a leaf PTE is modified and the corresponding virtual address is accessed without a subsuming SFENCE.VMA having been executed in between, either the new translation or any older translation since the last subsuming SFENCE.VMA was executed will be used. It is unpredictable which translation will be chosen from that set, and subsequent accesses to the same virtual address might use different translations from that set. The behavior of such accesses is otherwise well defined.

- This property applies even if the virtual-address width for that translation differs from the width currently specified by satp.MODE. For a given virtual address and ASID, any translation since the last subsuming SFENCE.VMA might be used, even if that translation used a virtual address of a different width. For a given virtual address, any global translation since the last subsuming SFENCE.VMA might be used, regardless of both ASID and virtual-address width.

- Implementations must only perform implicit reads of the translation data structures pointed to by the current contents of the satp register or a subsequent valid (V=1) translation data structure entry.

- Changing satp.MODE from Bare to other modes and vice versa takes effect immediately, without the need to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction.

- Changes to satp.ASID take effect immediately.

- When software recycles an ASID, i.e., reassociates it with a different page table, it should first change satp to point to the new page table using the recycled ASID, then execute SFENCE.VMA with rs1=x0 and rs2 set to the recycled ASID.

- When software recycles an ASID, software can alternatively execute the same SFENCE.VMA instruction while a different ASID is loaded into satp, provided the next time satp is loaded with the recycled ASID, it is simultaneously loaded with the new page table.

- If the implementation does not provide ASIDs, or software chooses to always use ASID 0, then after every satp write, software should execute SFENCE.VMA with rs1=x0.

- If the implementation does not provide ASIDs, or software chooses to always use ASID 0, and no global translations have been modified, rs2 should be set to a register other than x0 but which contains the value zero, so that global translations are not flushed.

- If a hart employs an address-translation cache, that cache must appear to be private to that hart. In particular, the meaning of an ASID is local to a hart; software may choose to use the same ASID to refer to different address spaces on different harts.

- For implementations that make satp.MODE read-only zero, and therefore always Bare, attempts to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction might raise an illegal-instruction exception.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: If a leaf PTE is modified and the corresponding virtual address is accessed without an intervening subsuming SFENCE.VMA, either the new translation or any older translation since the last subsuming SFENCE.VMA was executed will be used, even if that translation uses a virtual-address width different from the width currently specified by satp.MODE.

RULE 2: If a leaf PTE is modified and the corresponding virtual address is accessed without an intervening subsuming SFENCE.VMA, it is unpredictable whether satp-based translation will select the new translation or an older translation since the last subsuming SFENCE.VMA was executed, even if the translations use different virtual-address widths.

RULE 3: If a leaf PTE is modified and the corresponding virtual address is accessed without an intervening subsuming SFENCE.VMA, subsequent accesses to that virtual address might use different translations from the permitted set, even if a translation uses a virtual-address width different from the width currently specified by satp.MODE.

RULE 4: If a leaf PTE is modified and the corresponding virtual address is accessed without an intervening subsuming SFENCE.VMA, the behavior of the access is otherwise well defined even if the selected translation uses a virtual-address width different from the width currently specified by satp.MODE.

RULE 5: For a given virtual address and ASID, any translation since the last subsuming SFENCE.VMA might be used even if that translation used a virtual-address width different from the width currently specified by satp.MODE.

RULE 6: For a given virtual address, any global translation since the last subsuming SFENCE.VMA might be used regardless of satp.ASID and virtual-address width.

RULE 7: An implementation MUST perform implicit reads only of translation data structures pointed to by the current contents of satp or by a subsequent valid translation data structure entry with V=1.

RULE 8: Changing satp.MODE from Bare to a non-Bare mode takes effect immediately without requiring execution of an SFENCE.VMA instruction.

RULE 9: Changing satp.MODE from a non-Bare mode to Bare takes effect immediately without requiring execution of an SFENCE.VMA instruction.

RULE 10: Changes to satp.ASID take effect immediately.

RULE 11: When software recycles an ASID by reassociating it with a different page table, software SHOULD first change satp to point to the new page table using the recycled ASID and then execute SFENCE.VMA with rs1=x0 and rs2 set to the recycled ASID.

RULE 12: When software recycles an ASID, software can execute SFENCE.VMA with rs1=x0 and rs2 set to the recycled ASID while a different ASID is loaded into satp, provided that the next load of the recycled ASID into satp simultaneously loads the new page table into satp.

RULE 13: If the implementation does not provide ASIDs or software always uses ASID 0, software SHOULD execute SFENCE.VMA with rs1=x0 after every write to satp.

RULE 14: If the implementation does not provide ASIDs or software always uses ASID 0, and no global translations have been modified, software SHOULD execute the SFENCE.VMA following each satp write with rs1=x0 and rs2 set to a register other than x0 that contains zero so that global translations are not flushed.

RULE 15: If a hart employs an address-translation cache for satp-based address translation, that cache MUST appear to be private to that hart.

RULE 16: If a hart employs an address-translation cache, the meaning of satp.ASID is local to that hart.

RULE 17: Software may use the same satp.ASID value to refer to different address spaces on different harts.

RULE 18: In an implementation where satp.MODE is read-only zero, satp is always in Bare mode.

RULE 19: In an implementation where satp.MODE is read-only zero and satp is therefore always in Bare mode, an attempt to execute an SFENCE.VMA instruction might raise an illegal-instruction exception.

The following description:

- When Sv32 virtual memory mode is selected in the MODE field of the satp register, supervisor virtual addresses are translated into supervisor physical addresses via a two-level page table. The 20-bit virtual page number (VPN) is translated into a 22-bit physical page number (PPN), while the 12-bit page offset is untranslated. The resulting supervisor-level physical addresses are then checked using any physical memory protection structures before being directly converted to machine-level physical addresses. If necessary, supervisor-level physical addresses are zero-extended to the number of physical address bits found in the implementation.

- For an RV32 system supporting 34 bits of physical address, when the value of satp.MODE is Sv32, a 34-bit physical address is produced directly, and therefore no zero extension is needed.

- For an RV32 system supporting 34 bits of physical address, when the value of satp.MODE is Bare, the 32-bit virtual address is translated unmodified into a 32-bit physical address, and then that physical address is zero-extended into a 34-bit machine-level physical address.

- The physical page number of the root page table is stored in the satp register.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: When Sv32 virtual memory mode is selected in the MODE field of satp, supervisor virtual addresses are translated into supervisor physical addresses through a two-level page table.
RULE 2: When Sv32 virtual memory mode is selected in the MODE field of satp, the 20-bit virtual page number is translated into a 22-bit physical page number.
RULE 3: When Sv32 virtual memory mode is selected in the MODE field of satp, the 12-bit page offset is untranslated.
RULE 4: When Sv32 virtual memory mode is selected in the MODE field of satp, each resulting supervisor-level physical address is checked using any physical memory protection structures before being directly converted to a machine-level physical address.
RULE 5: When Sv32 virtual memory mode is selected in the MODE field of satp, supervisor-level physical addresses are zero-extended to the number of physical address bits found in the implementation if necessary.
RULE 6: On an RV32 system supporting 34 bits of physical address, when satp.MODE is Sv32, translation directly produces a 34-bit physical address.
RULE 7: On an RV32 system supporting 34 bits of physical address, when satp.MODE is Sv32, no zero extension of the resulting 34-bit physical address is needed.
RULE 8: On an RV32 system supporting 34 bits of physical address, when satp.MODE is Bare, the 32-bit virtual address is translated unmodified into a 32-bit physical address.
RULE 9: On an RV32 system supporting 34 bits of physical address, when satp.MODE is Bare, the resulting 32-bit physical address is zero-extended into a 34-bit machine-level physical address.
RULE 10: The physical page number of the root page table is stored in satp.

The following description:

1. At the start of virtual address translation, let `a = satp.ppn * PAGESIZE`, and let `i = LEVELS-1`. For Sv32, `PAGESIZE = 2^12` and `LEVELS = 2`.

2. The `satp` register must be active, i.e., the effective privilege mode must be S-mode or U-mode.

3. Implementations may execute the address-translation algorithm speculatively at any time, for any virtual address, as long as `satp` is active, as defined in Section 12.1.11. Such speculative executions have the effect of pre-populating the address-translation cache.

4. Speculative executions of the address-translation algorithm behave as non-speculative executions of the algorithm do, except that they must not set the dirty bit for a PTE, they must not trigger an exception, and they must not create address-translation cache entries if those entries would have been invalidated by any `SFENCE.VMA` instruction executed by the hart since the speculative execution of the algorithm began.

5. The address-translation algorithm may only touch page tables reachable from the root page table indicated in `satp`. Therefore, the range of addresses that an implementation's page-table walker will touch is fully under supervisor control.

6. Although it would be uncommon to place page tables reachable from the root page table indicated in `satp` in non-idempotent memory, there is no explicit prohibition against doing so.

7. The address-translation algorithm does not admit the possibility of ignoring high-order PPN bits for implementations with narrower physical addresses.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: At the start of virtual address translation, a equals satp.ppn * PAGESIZE, where PAGESIZE equals 2^12 for Sv32.

RULE 2: For virtual address translation, satp must be active, meaning that the effective privilege mode must be S-mode or U-mode.

RULE 3: While satp is active, speculative execution of the address-translation algorithm behaves as non-speculative execution except for setting a PTE dirty bit, triggering an exception, and creating an address-translation cache entry that would have been invalidated by an SFENCE.VMA instruction executed by the hart since the speculative execution began.

RULE 4: While satp is active, speculative execution of the address-translation algorithm must not set the dirty bit for a PTE.

RULE 5: While satp is active, speculative execution of the address-translation algorithm must not trigger an exception.

RULE 6: While satp is active, speculative execution of the address-translation algorithm must not create an address-translation cache entry if the entry would have been invalidated by any SFENCE.VMA instruction executed by the hart since the speculative execution began.

RULE 7: The address-translation algorithm may only touch page tables reachable from the root page table indicated in satp.

RULE 8: The range of addresses that a page-table walker will touch through page tables reachable from the root page table indicated in satp is fully under supervisor control.

RULE 9: There is no explicit prohibition against placing page tables reachable from the root page table indicated in satp in non-idempotent memory.

RULE 10: The address-translation algorithm that starts from the root page table indicated in satp does not admit the possibility of ignoring high-order PPN bits for implementations with narrower physical addresses.

The following description:

- The vsatp register is a VSXLEN-bit read/write register that is VS-mode's version of supervisor register satp.

- When V=1, vsatp substitutes for the usual satp, so instructions that normally read or modify satp actually access vsatp instead.

- When V=0, a write to vsatp with an unsupported MODE value is either ignored as it is for satp, or the fields of vsatp are treated as WARL in the normal way.

- When V=1, a write to satp with an unsupported MODE value is ignored and no write to vsatp is effected.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: The vsatp register is a VSXLEN-bit read/write register that is VS-mode's version of the supervisor register satp.
RULE 2: When V=1, vsatp substitutes for the usual satp.
RULE 3: When V=1, an instruction that normally reads satp accesses vsatp instead.
RULE 4: When V=1, an instruction that normally modifies satp accesses vsatp instead.
RULE 5: When V=0, a write to vsatp with an unsupported MODE value is either ignored as a write with an unsupported MODE value is ignored for satp, or the fields of vsatp are treated as WARL in the normal way.
RULE 6: When V=1, a write to satp with an unsupported MODE value is ignored.
RULE 7: When V=1, a write to satp with an unsupported MODE value does not effect a write to vsatp.

The following description:

- For virtual addresses, the ignore transformation replaces the upper PMLEN bits with the sign extension of the PMLEN+1st bit:

```text
transformed_effective_address =
{{PMLEN{effective_address[XLEN-PMLEN-1]}},
 effective_address[XLEN-PMLEN-1:0]}
```

- When applied to a physical address, including guest-physical addresses (i.e., all cases except when the active satp register's MODE field != Bare), the ignore transformation replaces the upper PMLEN bits with 0:

```text
transformed_effective_address =
{{PMLEN{0}}, effective_address[XLEN-PMLEN-1:0]}
```

This includes both the case of running in M-mode and running in other privilege modes with Bare address translation mode.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: When the active satp register's MODE field != Bare and the effective address is virtual, the ignore transformation replaces the upper PMLEN bits with the sign extension of effective_address[XLEN-PMLEN-1], producing transformed_effective_address = {{PMLEN{effective_address[XLEN-PMLEN-1]}}, effective_address[XLEN-PMLEN-1:0]}.

RULE 2: In all physical-address cases, including guest-physical-address cases, other than when the active satp register's MODE field != Bare, the ignore transformation replaces the upper PMLEN bits with zero, producing transformed_effective_address = {{PMLEN{0}}, effective_address[XLEN-PMLEN-1:0]}.

RULE 3: When running in M-mode, the case is included among the physical-address cases distinguished from the case where the active satp register's MODE field != Bare, and the ignore transformation replaces the upper PMLEN bits with zero.

RULE 4: When running in a privilege mode other than M-mode with the active satp register's MODE field equal to Bare, the ignore transformation replaces the upper PMLEN bits of the physical effective address with zero.

The following description:

- The counter-enable (scounteren) CSR is a 32-bit register that controls the availability of the hardware performance monitoring counters to U-mode.

- When the CY bit in scounteren is clear, attempts to read the cycle register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception. When the CY bit is set, access to the corresponding register is permitted.

- When the TM bit in scounteren is clear, attempts to read the time register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception. When the TM bit is set, access to the corresponding register is permitted.

- When the IR bit in scounteren is clear, attempts to read the instret register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception. When the IR bit is set, access to the corresponding register is permitted.

- When the HPMn bit in scounteren is clear, attempts to read the hpmcountern register while executing in U-mode will cause an illegal-instruction exception. When the HPMn bit is set, access to the corresponding register is permitted.

- scounteren must be implemented.

- Any of the bits in scounteren may be read-only zero, indicating reads to the corresponding counter will cause an exception when executing in U-mode. Hence, the bits are effectively WARL fields.

- The setting of a bit in mcounteren does not affect whether the corresponding bit in scounteren is writable.

- U-mode may only access a counter if the corresponding bits in scounteren and mcounteren are both set.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: The counter-enable scounteren CSR is a 32-bit register.
RULE 2: The scounteren CSR controls the availability of hardware performance monitoring counters to U-mode.
RULE 3: When the CY bit in scounteren is clear, an attempt to read the cycle register while executing in U-mode causes an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 4: When the CY bit in scounteren is set, U-mode access to the cycle register is permitted.
RULE 5: When the TM bit in scounteren is clear, an attempt to read the time register while executing in U-mode causes an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 6: When the TM bit in scounteren is set, U-mode access to the time register is permitted.
RULE 7: When the IR bit in scounteren is clear, an attempt to read the instret register while executing in U-mode causes an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 8: When the IR bit in scounteren is set, U-mode access to the instret register is permitted.
RULE 9: When an HPMn bit in scounteren is clear, an attempt to read the corresponding hpmcountern register while executing in U-mode causes an illegal-instruction exception.
RULE 10: When an HPMn bit in scounteren is set, U-mode access to the corresponding hpmcountern register is permitted.
RULE 11: The scounteren CSR must be implemented.
RULE 12: Any bit in scounteren may be read-only zero.
RULE 13: A read-only-zero bit in scounteren indicates that reading the corresponding counter while executing in U-mode causes an exception.
RULE 14: The bits in scounteren are effectively WARL fields.
RULE 15: Setting a bit in mcounteren does not affect whether the corresponding bit in scounteren is writable.
RULE 16: U-mode may access a counter only if the corresponding bits in scounteren and mcounteren are both set.
======================================================= END - 73 =======================================================

The following description:

- When V=1, a virtual-instruction exception (code 22) is normally raised instead of an illegal-instruction exception if the attempted instruction is HS-qualified but is prevented from executing when V=1 either due to insufficient privilege or because the instruction is expressly disabled by a supervisor or hypervisor CSR such as scounteren or hcounteren. An instruction is HS-qualified if it would be valid to execute in HS-mode, for some values of the instruction's register operands, assuming fields TSR and TVM of CSR mstatus are both zero.

- In VU-mode, a virtual-instruction exception is raised for attempts to access a non-high-half counter CSR when the corresponding bit in either hcounteren or scounteren is 0 and the same bit in mcounteren is 1.

- In VU-mode, if XLEN=32, a virtual-instruction exception is raised for attempts to access a high-half counter CSR when the corresponding bit in either hcounteren or scounteren is 0 and the same bit in mcounteren is 1.
============================================================
=========
RESPONSE:
=========
============================================================
RULE 1: When V=1, a virtual-instruction exception with code 22 is normally raised instead of an illegal-instruction exception if scounteren expressly disables an attempted HS-qualified instruction, where an instruction is HS-qualified if it would be valid to execute in HS-mode for some values of its register operands when the TSR and TVM fields of mstatus are both zero.

RULE 2: In VU-mode, an attempt to access a non-high-half counter CSR raises a virtual-instruction exception when the corresponding bit in either hcounteren or scounteren is 0 and the same bit in mcounteren is 1.

RULE 3: In VU-mode when XLEN=32, an attempt to access a high-half counter CSR raises a virtual-instruction exception when the corresponding bit in either hcounteren or scounteren is 0 and the same bit in mcounteren is 1.
